- `2026-07-01 in force (Act No. 21 of 2026); prior amendment 2025-07-01 (Act No. 43 of 2025) | tax | ENACTED: the Real Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2026 (No. 21 of 2026, assent 30 Jun 2026, Extraordinary Official Gazette 1 Jul 2026, s.1(2) "This Act shall come into force on the 1st day of July, 2026") enacted the former Bill 2026-0028 and amends the Real Property Tax Act Ch. 375 as previously amended by Act No. 43 of 2025. (1) New "foreign owner-occupied property" class: property owned by a non-Bahamian who holds a residence permit or annual work permit under the Immigration Act (Ch. 191) or a Home Owner Resident Permit under the International Persons Landholding Act (Ch. 140) and "occupies and resides in such property exclusively as a dwelling house on a permanent or seasonal basis" — taxed at "five-eighths of one per centum" (0.625%) on the part of the value up to $1,000,000 and 1% on the part above $1,000,000, with the maximum annual tax not to exceed $200,000 (no exempt first slice in the Act text). (2) "owner-occupied property" is restricted to a "Bahamian or the holder of certificate of permanent residence with a right to engage in gainful occupation", and the words "for a period of not less than 90 days in the year" are deleted. (3) The owner-occupied cap proviso is replaced by "the maximum annual tax rate shall not exceed one hundred and fifty thousand dollars" — the 183-day condition that the 2025 Act attached to the cap is gone. (4) s.42A(1) first-time-homeowner exemption now reads "Bahamian" person; s.42A(6) figure $500,000.00 → $600,000.00 — for a foreign buyer the Bahamian-only restriction is the operative change. (5) New s.3A: jointly owned property whose owners fall in different categories is apportioned equally and each share is taxed under its own owner's category. (6) New ss.25A–25Y power of sale: the Treasurer may sell property whose tax has been in arrears for more than twelve months, and these sections "shall not apply to owner-occupied property beneficially owned by a Bahamian" — foreign-owned property is within scope; new s.25Z also lets the Treasurer acquire property other than Bahamian-owned owner-occupied property where tax plus penalties and interest exceed the assessed value, with the assessed value applied to the arrears. (7) New s.3(2)(bb) proviso: in a duplex, triplex or fourplex the unit classified owner-occupied under s.3(8B) is taxed at the owner-occupied rate and the aggregate annual tax on the remaining unit or units may not exceed $300,000. The FY2026/27 Budget Communication's "former 180-day test" wording never had a statutory counterpart (the 2025 Act said 90 and 183) — do not carry 180 days. The official tabled-bills page still lists "Real Property Tax (Amendment) Bill, 2026" (a list of bills tabled in 2026, not evidence of non-enactment). WARNING: DIR RPT FAQ Q10 (page last modified 28 Apr 2023, unchanged on 2026-10-21) still lists only owner-occupied (1st $300,000 exempt / 0.625% next $200,000 / 1% above $500,000), residential, commercial-or-foreign-owned-rental and vacant-land-for-foreigners bands, with no foreign owner-occupied category — DIR guidance is behind the 2026 Act, so confirm classification and bands with DIR in writing before any carry-cost or transaction-cost computation | Real Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2026 No. 21 of 2026 https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/AMENDING/2026/2026-0021A/2026-0021A.pdf + Real Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2025 No. 43 of 2025 https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/AMENDING/2025/2025-0043A/2025-0043A.pdf + consolidated index https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/legislation/index/by-alphabetical.html + tabled bills https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/legislation/tabled-in-parliament/tabled-in-house-of-assembly.html + FY2026-27 Budget Communication https://bahamasbudget.gov.bs + DIR RPT FAQ https://inlandrevenue.finance.gov.bs/real-property-tax/faqs-rpt/ | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --tax --home-tax`

- `2025-10-30 | foreign-buyer | CCJ ENDED its decade-long monitoring of the 2015 Consent Order at the Final Monitoring Hearing on 30 Oct 2025. CCJ President Winston Anderson: "That supervision ends today", citing the January 2022 Maya of Southern Belize Free Prior and Informed Consultation Protocol and "the adoption in September 2025 of a working draft on Recognition and Protection of Maya Customary Land Tenure in Southern Belize, along with the formulation of drafting instructions and the selection/engagement of a draftsperson". The Court closed its MONITORING role only, not its jurisdiction — "The Court will, of course, remain of service to both sides in its regular capacity". Subject-specific legislation is still NOT enacted: the Court said "The ultimate goal was to have subject-specific legislation enacted" and that its hope is that implementation of the 2015 Order "will proceed through its legislative and other phases". On 30 Jul 2026 the CCJ dismissed the Maya Leaders Alliance / Toledo Alcaldes Association application filed 14 May 2026 in BZCV2014/002 seeking clarification of the 2015 Consent Order. Its written reasons ([2026] CCJ 13 (AJ) BZ, dated 2 Oct 2026) name the draft as the Maya Customary Land Tenure (Recognition and Protection of Rights) Bill, 2025, record that the Working Draft went to a legal draftsperson on 11 Sep 2025 and the Draft Bill to a Review Panel of the parties' representatives on 21 Oct 2025, state "The Draft Bill has yet to be laid before, debated or enacted by the National Assembly", conclude that the Court "ought not to intervene at this point in the way asked for by the Appellants", and say that whether the Draft Bill, if enacted, is consistent with ss 3, 3(d), 16 and 17 of the Constitution is justiciable by way of constitutional challenge. At the 30 Jul hearing Justice Anderson said "The precise scope and extent of Maya Customary Land Tenure will therefore necessarily will remain inchoate and uncertain until authoritatively identified and codified by the laws of Belize" (⚠️ 7News Belize 30 Jul 2026 — secondary; the phrase is not in the written reasons). Minister of Indigenous Peoples Affairs Dr Louis Zabaneh, at a National Consultation and Information-Sharing Session in Punta Gorda Town, said the draft "now goes through a review panel where we have membership from the government on the panel, from the MLA /TAA and from private land owners" before returning to Cabinet and only then going to the National Assembly (⚠️ Love FM 5 Aug 2026 — secondary). After the reasons were released, Mr E Andrew Marshalleck Jr SC, counsel for the Attorney General, said the review panel would be scheduled "as quickly as we can get the group together", and the Maya Leaders Alliance said it had proposed changes to the draft, with another review panel meeting expected in early November 2026 (⚠️ Love FM and Greater Belize Media 6 Oct 2026 — secondary). The National Assembly Draft Bills 2026 register listed no Maya, indigenous or customary-land-tenure Bill when last read on 2026-08-07 (16 bills listed); the nationalassembly.gov.bz host returned 'This Account has been suspended' on 2026-10-21. Buyer position unchanged: Maya customary land tenure is "a constitutionally protected right" per Maya Leaders Alliance v Attorney General of Belize [2015] CCJ 15 (AJ), but communal title for the Maya villages of the Toledo District is still not fixed in statute (2026 data) — the boundary between registered private title and customary tenure remains unlegislated, so confirm current Bill/Act status against the National Assembly and a Belize attorney before any Toledo-District deal | CCJ President's Closing Remarks at the Final Monitoring Hearing 30 Oct 2025 — `https://ccj.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Anderson_20251030.pdf` + CCJ Reasons for Decision [2026] CCJ 13 (AJ) BZ, 2 Oct 2026 — `https://ccj.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/2026_CCJ_13_AJ_BZ.pdf` + National Assembly of Belize Draft Bills 2026 register — `https://www.nationalassembly.gov.bz/draft-bills-2026` + (⚠️ media) 7News Belize 30 Jul 2026 — `https://www.7newsbelize.com/printstory.php?func=print&nid=79319` + (⚠️ media) Love FM Belize 5 Aug 2026 — `https://lovefm.com/government-considers-expanding-maya-land-rights-consultations-nationwide` + (⚠️ media) Love FM Belize 6 Oct 2026 — `https://lovefm.com/ccj-declines-to-intervene-in-maya-customary-land-rights-legislation` + (⚠️ media) Greater Belize Media 6 Oct 2026 — `https://www.greaterbelize.com/ccj-sends-maya-land-fight-back-to-belizean-lawmakers` | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --visa ownership --notary`

- `2001-08-30 | foreign-buyer | ENFORCEMENT-TREND CLAIM WITHDRAWN and replaced with the statutory position. Two verification passes (2026-08-07) located no MLMUPC or GDCG prakas, circular or statement evidencing a 2024-2026 anti-nominee campaign, and the US State Department 2025 Investment Climate Statement for Cambodia does not use the word nominee anywhere in the report as fetched 2026-08-07 (0 occurrences again on 2026-10-21; the 2026 ICS, PDF dated September 2026, also has none) — so "under enforcement pressure" and "increasingly challenged" were unsourced and are removed. The statute is the stronger claim and is primary-quotable. Constitution Art. 44: "Only natural persons or legal entities of Khmer nationality shall have the right to own land." Land Law 2001 (Royal Kram NS/RKM/0801/14, promulgated 30 August 2001) Art. 9 expressly PERMITS a company with 51 percent or more Cambodian-held shares to own land, but adds: "Only percentages stipulated in the articles of incorporation are taken into account. Any private agreement signed by a shareholder that is contrary to this article is null and void." The side-agreement, share-pledge and power-of-attorney bundle marketed as making a nominee land-holding company safe for the foreign funder is therefore VOID BY STATUTE from the day it is signed, not merely exposed to enforcement risk — the structure is legal, the foreign buyer's protection inside it is not. Land Law Art. 8: a foreigner who "falsifies national identity to become an owner of land in Cambodia shall be punished as determined under article 251" (Art. 251: "imprisonment from one (1) to five (5) years") and "Any property bought under these circumstances will be seized as state property without compensation from the state." CITE Land Law Art. 9, NOT Sub-Decree 111 Art. 3.2 — Sub-Decree 111 (2005) is a QIP-incentives instrument and its successor Sub-Decree 139 of 26 June 2023 carries no Use of Nominees clause (0 occurrences of nominee, re-checked 2026-10-21). The Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code (2011) Art. 80, which omits or rewrites many Land Law articles, leaves Arts. 8, 9 and 251 untouched. WATCH (corrected 2026-10-21): the 2026 ICS says "Cambodia is drafting a new land law which would include articles" requiring landowners to be Cambodian citizens, clarifying tax payment obligations, detailing co-ownership rights and promoting a digital cadastral system (the 2025 ICS said it was "still undergoing stakeholder consultation"); draft text HAS circulated — Open Development Cambodia (NGO data hub, secondary) hosts three drafts listing MLMUPC as issuer (2023 1st draft, 26 March 2024 2nd draft, 19 June 2024 3rd draft), contents not reviewed here; no Council of Ministers or National Assembly adoption located — no forecast made | Constitution of the Kingdom of Cambodia Art. 44 via Ministry of Foreign Affairs and International Cooperation `https://mfaic.gov.kh/publish/files/uploads/2Z87AYOO63VI/Constitution%20of%20the%20Kingdom%20of%20Cambodia.pdf` + Land Law 2001 Arts. 8, 9 and 251 via `https://asset.cambodia.gov.kh/mowa/2016/12/land-law-2001-kh-eng1.pdf` (unofficial English translation, Khmer version official) + Council for the Development of Cambodia `https://cdc.gov.kh/laws-and-regulations/land` + Sub-Decree 139 of 26 June 2023 `https://cib-cdc.gov.kh/media/2025/04/2.-Sub-Decree-139-on-Implementing-LOI-2023-EN.pdf` + US State Department 2025 Investment Climate Statement Cambodia `https://www.state.gov/reports/2025-investment-climate-statements/cambodia` + US State Department 2026 Investment Climate Statement Cambodia `https://www.state.gov/reports/2026-investment-climate-statements/cambodia` + Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code via CDC `https://cdc.gov.kh/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/Law-on-Implementation-of-CC_Eng_FINAL_110531.pdf` + Open Development Cambodia draft Land Law record (secondary) `https://data.opendevelopmentcambodia.net/laws_record/draft-land-law` | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --visa ownership --integrity`
- `2019-01-02 (Royal Kram; constitutionally untested) | foreign-buyer | Law on Trust (Royal Kram NS/RKM/0119/002, made 02 Jan 2019; adopted by the National Assembly 13 Dec 2018; declared urgent by Art. 57) over land — whether a foreign-beneficiary trust over land survives the Constitution Art. 44 bar on foreign land ownership is untested: no Constitutional Council decision located (2026-10-21). The Law's own Art. 16 caps an individual-interest trust at 100 years and provides that if, on termination, the beneficiary "has not received the permission or the rights in possession of the property which is the land, then the trust property must become the tenancy until the beneficiary has the full right in possessing the land in accordance with the applicable law" — the statute defers to land-ownership law at termination rather than overriding it. The unofficial English translation contains no reference to foreigners or nationality (glossary: Beneficiary = "a person who benefits from the Trust"). US State Department 2026 ICS: the Trust Law allows "investors to own or transfer landed property via a regulated trust mechanism", the market is "still developing and subject to regulatory updates", and 1,712 trusts worth about $2.5 billion were registered in 2025. The Trust Regulator site lists 2026 prakas and guidelines (dispute resolution, branches, AML sanctions, ethics, trustee operations, customer due diligence) — none located on land or foreign beneficiaries. Do NOT rely on a trust as a route to foreign land ownership until tested | Law on Trust (unofficial English translation) via Trust Regulator `https://www.trustregulator.gov.kh/storage/lawregulations/January2024/Ko8e0it5UbPMSXND7No2.pdf` + Trust Regulator `https://www.trustregulator.gov.kh/` + US State Department 2026 Investment Climate Statement Cambodia `https://www.state.gov/reports/2026-investment-climate-statements/cambodia` | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 2 | --visa ownership --integrity`

A commencement order — Statutory Instrument No. 38 of 2026, Citizenship (Amendment) Act (Date of Commencement) Order, 2026 (Ministry of Labour and Home Affairs) — is listed among papers received for tabling on Parliament's Business Update for the 3rd Meeting of the 2nd Session of the 13th Parliament (PDF dated 26 Aug 2026); the commencement date and whether it commences the whole Act or only parts are data not publicly available — verify at the Government Gazette / MLHA. A media report of a ministerial briefing (secondary, ~20 Aug 2026) says multiple citizenship is being rolled out while the Citizenship by Investment Programme "remains on hold until the end of the year". Consequential National Registration (Amendment) Bill No. 35 of 2025 recorded as Passed on Parliament's Bills register (bill date 2026-04-23). NO qualifying investment threshold is gazetted — the Minister told Parliament at the Dec 2025 second reading he was "not in a position to state the amount"; the US$75,000-90,000 figure in circulation comes from promoter Arton Capital's pre-registration portal and is NOT an official rate. CBI still NOT operational — no launch notice located. Economic citizens are barred from voting, public office, free government education, government funding and state/tribal land allocation. - `2025-02-03 | visa | EU PERMANENTLY revoked Vanuatu's visa-free Schengen access — Regulation (EU) 2025/11 of 19 Dec 2024 (Council decision 12 Dec 2024; OJ L 14 Jan 2025; enters into force on the twentieth day after publication) moved Vanuatu from Annex II Part 1 to Annex I Part 1 of Reg (EU) 2018/1806, and the permanent visa requirement APPLIES FROM 3 Feb 2025 (Danish Immigration Service, page updated 2025-02-03). Chain: partial suspension from 4 May 2022 for ordinary passports issued as of 25 May 2015 (Council Decision (EU) 2022/366 of 3 Mar 2022) → full suspension of the visa-waiver Agreement by Council Decision (EU) 2022/2198 of 8 Nov 2022 → visa required 4 Feb 2023 (Commission Delegated Reg (EU) 2023/222 of 1 Dec 2022, suspension running to 3 Feb 2025) → permanent from 3 Feb 2025. The first use of the suspension clause in the EU visa-exemption rules (European Parliament press release 21 Nov 2024), and STILL IN FORCE — EUR-Lex lists Reg (EU) 2025/11 as in force and DG HOME's visa-policy page states the visa waiver agreement with Vanuatu has been suspended under the suspension mechanism; the same page notes the mechanism was revised by Reg (EU) 2025/2441 of 26 Nov 2025 (OJ 10 Dec 2025), whose grounds include operation of an investor citizenship scheme. CBI CORRECTED: the DSP and CIIP donation routes are not property-linked (the receiving fund is named the National Development Fund by secondary sources only), BUT the Vanuatu Citizenship Office lists a property-linked Real Estate Option Program (REO) among its current citizenship types — commenced 23 Jul 2021 under s.13F Citizenship Act [Cap 112] and the Citizenship (Real Estate Option Program) Regulation Order No. 93 of 2021; the applicant must purchase a property in a Vanuatu Government Approved Project (four listed: Pacific Springs, Narpow Point Coral Bay and FPF Rainbow City Project on Efate; Milai on Espiritu Santo); no suspension or closure notice on the Office's pages; REO minimum price not published by the Office — data not publicly available, verify at the Vanuatu Citizenship Office. ### 🇹🇹 TT

- `2024 (enforced); halted 2025 | tax | Property Tax reintroduced 2024 and then HALTED by the 2025 government. Statutory residential rate is 2% of Annual Taxable Value (ATV = Annual Rental Value less a 10% voids deduction), i.e. ≈1.8% effective on ARV. The Property Tax (Amendment) Act 2024 (Act No. 3 of 2024, Gazette Legal Supplement Part A Vol. 63 No. 53, assented 27 Mar 2024) WAS enacted and cut the residential rate 3% → 2%; IRD declared every Notice of Assessment dated before 6 Mar 2024 null and void and reissued at 2%, payment due 30 Sep 2024. COLLECTION HALTED: the FY2026 Budget Statement (13 Oct 2025, p.22) states "We halted the Property Tax that created anxiety for thousands of people, including the elderly, retired and low-income" (same words in the House Hansard of 13 Oct 2025); the May 2025 announcement of a Property Tax Act repeal is reported in the press only (secondary) and a ttparliament.org search this pass found no Property Tax repeal Bill or Act, so the statute and its 2% rate are read as still on the books while collection is halted — the legal instrument of the halt is not published at a primary source read this pass (data not publicly available — verify at IRD / Ministry of Finance). Budget 2026 (13 Oct 2025) added a separate Landlord Business Surcharge on rental income from 1 Jan 2026 — a levy on landlords, not a property-tax rate. Non-residential statutory rates were not amended by the 2024 Act: commercial 5%, agricultural 1%, industrial 6% where plant and machinery is housed in a building and 3% where it is not (2018 MoF statement to the House — re-verify against the Act before relying on them). Before any carry-cost computation, confirm with IRD whether any property tax is being assessed or collected for the purchase year and whether arrears from 2024 notices remain a charge on the property | FY2026 Budget Statement https://www.finance.gov.tt/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/Budget-Statement-FY-2026-1.pdf + House Hansard 13 Oct 2025 https://www.ttparliament.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/hh2025013u.pdf + https://www.ird.gov.tt/news/amended-property-tax-notices-of-assessment + tax.mygovtt.com + Ministry of Finance media release 18 Nov 2023 | 2026-09-24 | 2026-11-15 | 1 | --tax (TT)`

- `2026-01-22 | ownership | Law on Non-Saudis Ownership of Real Estate (نظام تملك غير السعوديين للعقار), Royal Decree M/14 of 19/1/1447H (14 Jul 2025) on Council of Ministers Decision 42 of 13/1/1447H (8 Jul 2025); REGA lists its publication date as 30/1/1447H (25 Jul 2025); IN FORCE 22 Jan 2026 — REGA's own notice announces entry into force from Thursday 3 Sha'ban 1447H = 22 Jan 2026 (art. 15: 180 days after gazette publication), which settles the 21-vs-22 Jan split. Art. 14: it replaces the 2000 Law of Real Estate Ownership and Investment by Non-Saudis (RD M/15 of 17/4/1421H) and repeals conflicting provisions; the decree also repeals RD 44 of 29/11/1377H and preserves (a) real-estate rights lawfully acquired by non-Saudis before entry into force and (b) existing statutory bans on ownership in specified places; CoM Decision 42 item 4 orders the steps to cancel art. 4 of the GCC-citizens ownership regulation (RD M/22 of 3/4/1432H) as it relates to property inside Makkah and Madinah, effective from M/14's entry into force. REVERSES the prior assertion in `shared/notary-forced-heirship.md` that Mecca and Medina are closed to all non-Saudis: art. 2(4) limits the art. 2(1) right in the cities of Makkah and Madinah to Muslim natural persons (a non-Muslim may not take title); art. 2(3) lets a lawfully resident non-Saudi natural person own one residence outside the Cabinet-designated scope, Makkah and Madinah excepted (exec. reg. art. 7: spouse and non-Saudi descendants count as dependants until the marriage ends or the descendant turns 25). CORRECTED company limb (was 'unlisted foreign companies only for operational requirements or staff accommodation'): art. 3(1) lets an unlisted company incorporated under the Saudi Companies Law with non-Saudi shareholders own inside the designated scope INCLUDING Makkah and Madinah, with no MISA approval (exec. reg. art. 8(3)); outside the scope it may own only for its activities and staff housing, with MISA approval and EXCLUDING Makkah and Madinah (exec. reg. art. 8(1)-(2)); art. 4: listed companies, investment funds and licensed SPEs own anywhere including the two cities under Capital Market Authority rules; a non-Saudi company registers with MISA and discloses direct and indirect owners first (exec. reg. art. 3). IMPLEMENTATION: on 23 Jun 2026 (8/1/1448H) the Council of Ministers approved the executive regulation (Decision 43 of 8/1/1448H, Umm Al-Qura 18/1/1448H = 3 Jul 2026) and the geographic scopes in which non-Saudis may own (scope maps with ownership percentages, right types and usufruct periods on REGA's Saudi Properties portal). FEE: law art. 9 caps a REGA fee on a non-Saudi's disposal of real rights at 5% of value; exec. reg. art. 9 sets 2% for the city of Riyadh, the city of Makkah, the city of Madinah and Jeddah governorate (all right types, all uses) and art. 10 zero-rates disposals elsewhere plus estate partition (قسمة التركة), final court orders, expropriation, gratuitous transfers to a waqf or public body and other listed cases. Whether a non-Muslim heir may take title to Makkah / Madinah property is not addressed in the law or the regulation text — data not publicly available — verify at REGA / Ministry of Justice. Verified 2026-10-21 | Umm Al-Qura law text https://www.uqn.gov.sa/details?p=27293 + REGA law page with decree and CoM decision https://rega.gov.sa/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%85%D8%A9-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%82%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%AA/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%85%D8%A9-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%84%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%AD-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%AF%D9%84%D8%A9/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%85%D8%A9/%D9%86%D8%B8%D8%A7%D9%85-%D8%AA%D9%85%D9%84%D9%83-%D8%BA%D9%8A%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B3%D8%B9%D9%88%D8%AF%D9%8A%D9%8A%D9%86-%D9%84%D9%84%D8%B9%D9%82%D8%A7%D8%B1/ + Umm Al-Qura executive regulation 3 Jul 2026 https://www.uqn.gov.sa/decisions-and-regulations/4001281 + Umm Al-Qura Council of Ministers session 23 Jun 2026 https://www.uqn.gov.sa/news/council-of-ministers/4001222 + REGA in-force notice 22 Jan 2026 https://rega.gov.sa/media-center/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%AE%D8%A8%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D8%B9%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%86%D8%A7%D8%AA/%D9%87%D9%8A%D8%A6%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B9%D9%82%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%84%D9%86-%D8%A8%D8%AF%D8%A1-%D9%86%D9%81%D8%A7%D8%B0-%D9%86%D8%B8%D8%A7%D9%85-%D8%AA%D9%85%D9%84-%D9%83-%D8%BA%D9%8A%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B3%D8%B9%D9%88%D8%AF%D9%8A%D9%8A%D9%86-%D9%84%D9%84%D8%B9%D9%82%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D8%B9%D8%AA%D8%A8%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%A7-%D9%85%D9%86-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%88%D9%85 + REGA programme page https://rega.gov.sa/rega-services/platforms/non-saudi-real-estate-ownership-in-saudi-arabia | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --notary --price (SA)`
- `2025-08-22 | tax | SA White Land Tax superseded — the law was amended by Royal Decree M/244 of 7/11/1446H after Council of Ministers approval on 29 Apr 2025 (1 Dhul-Qi'dah 1446), published Umm Al-Qura 12 May 2025 (14 Dhul-Qi'dah 1446), and renamed "نظام رسوم الأراضي البيضاء والعقارات الشاغرة" (White Land and Vacant Property Fees); amended art. 3: (1) annual fee of up to 10% of land value (was 2.5%), state property excluded; (2) the Minister sets the application scope and the area, with the parcel or the aggregate holding not below 5,000 m² (was 10,000 m²) — white-land exec. reg. art. 7(4) applies the 5,000 m² floor to one owner's aggregate vacant land within the city; (3) vacant buildings pay an annual fee as a share of fair rent (أجرة المثل) capped at 5% of property value, which the Council of Ministers may raise to 10% on the ministerial committee's proposal. Vacant-property executive regulation now ISSUED (was due within one year of the 12 May 2025 publication): ministerial decision 4700822503/1 of 26/11/1447H, Umm Al-Qura 28/11/1447H = 15 May 2026, in force on publication; art. 6(1)(a) settles the fee base — a share of fair rent, not above 5% of building value — so the share-of-rent vs share-of-value question is closed; a building counts as vacant after 6 months' non-use, continuous or cumulative, in the reference year (art. 7 per a full-text reproduction and MOMAH's FAQ); the fee applies only in zones the Minister announces after at least 12 months of monitoring vacancy and market criteria (arts 5, 9); MOMAH's FAQ (read 2026-10-21) says covered cities and zones will be announced later — no vacant-property zone announcement located as of 2026-10-21, verify at MOMAH; TRANSFER GATE: Council of Ministers Decision 263 of 12/3/1448H (Umm Al-Qura 22/3/1448H = 4 Sep 2026) bars documenting any real-estate disposal that transfers ownership of land subject to the law until payment of the fees due is verified, and requires non-transfer dispositions (e.g. mortgage) to be documented so as to protect the state's right to collect the fees — verified 2026-10-21 | https://laws.boe.gov.sa/BoeLaws/Laws/LawDetails/6932e745-0f61-4f25-9e4a-a9a700f21d6b/1 / https://momah.gov.sa/ar/node/14963 / https://momah.gov.sa/ar/node/14971 / https://www.uqn.gov.sa/details?p=27369 / https://www.uqn.gov.sa/decisions-and-regulations/4000949 / https://www.uqn.gov.sa/decisions-and-regulations/4000950 / https://idlelands.momah.gov.sa/ar/guide/86011 / https://www.uqn.gov.sa/decisions-and-regulations/4001765 | 2026-10-21 | 2027-01-19 | 1 | --tax --price --compare --exit (SA)`

- `2023-01-01 | tax | Aruba overdrachtsbelasting restructured by Landsverordening Belastingplan 2023 (AB 2022 no. 161, dated 20 Dec 2022, issued 21 Dec 2022, in force 1 Jan 2023). Confirmed 2026-10-21 (DIMP Belastingplan 2023 page as indexed, plus the Wolters Kluwer InView consolidation of the ordinance, secondary): (a) one schedule for every property — 3% on the value of an onroerende zaak up to Afl. 250.000 and 6% on the excess; (b) the tax now also covers acquisition of ECONOMIC ownership (art. 3(1) and 3a(1)); (c) it covers shares in a vastgoedvennootschap — a company at least 30% of whose assets are Aruban real property where that property wholly or mainly (per DIMP at least 70%) serves acquisition, disposal or exploitation (art. 3a(2)), with the share value floored at the grondbelasting value. ## EU-wide instruments — transposition and application dates

A **directive** binds all 27 member states but reaches a buyer only through national law; the transposition deadline is the date by which that national law must exist. A **regulation** applies directly, so its application date is the date it bites. Every row below was read off the instrument's own EUR-Lex text on the verified date — do not restate a deadline from memory.

| Instrument | Topic | Deadline / application | Status (verified 2026-07-31) | Affected sections |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **(EU) 2024/1275** (EPBD recast, directive) | Energy performance — zero-emission buildings, MEPS, mandatory renovation passports | Transposition **29 May 2026**; art. 17(15) (no financial incentives for stand-alone fossil-fuel boilers) was due earlier, **1 Jan 2025** | **Deadline passed, unmet everywhere** — on 15 Jul 2026 the Commission opened infringement procedures with letters of formal notice to **all 27** member states for failing to fully transpose; two months to respond, then a possible reasoned opinion ([DG ENER, 15 Jul 2026](https://energy.ec.europa.eu/news/commission-calls-eu-countries-transpose-reinforced-rules-energy-performance-buildings-2026-07-15_en)) | `--esg` `--rental` |
| **(EU) 2024/1028** (regulation) | Short-let data collection + registration harmonisation | Directly applicable, **applies 20 May 2026** — a single date, no later tranche; member states had to adopt the penalty provisions by the same date; Commission evaluation due by 20 May 2031 (art. 18) | In application since 20 May 2026 | `--rental` |
| **(EU) 2024/1640** (AMLD6, directive) | Beneficial-ownership registers + access rules; **art. 18 single access point to real-estate information** | Main transposition **10 Jul 2027**. Derogations (art. 78): art. 74 by 10 Jul 2025 · **arts. 11, 12, 13 and 15 — BO-register access, incl. legitimate-interest access — by 10 Jul 2026** · **art. 18 by 10 Jul 2029** | 10 Jul 2026 access tranche now due; main deadline pending | `--tax` (ownership) `--finance` |
| **(EU) 2023/2226** (DAC8, Council directive) | Crypto-asset reporting | Adopt + publish by **31 Dec 2025**, apply from **1 Jan 2026**; two later tranches — 31 Dec 2027 (apply 1 Jan 2028) and 31 Dec 2029 (apply 1 Jan 2030) | First tranche in application since 1 Jan 2026; per-country transposition not individually verified | `--tax` |
| **(EU) 2024/1689** (AI Act, regulation) | High-risk AI — incl. systems that **evaluate creditworthiness or establish a credit score** of natural persons (annex III, 5(b)), i.e. mortgage decisioning | Directly applicable. General application **2 Aug 2026**; chapters I–II from 2 Feb 2025; chapter III section 4, V, VII, XII + art. 78 from 2 Aug 2025; art. 6(1) (the annex I product-safety route) deferred to 2 Aug 2027. Annex III systems are high-risk under **art. 6(2)**, so they follow the general date | Annex III high-risk obligations apply from 2 Aug 2026 | `--finance` |
| **(EU) 2023/1791** (EED recast, directive) | Energy efficiency | Transposition **11 Oct 2025**; applies from 12 Oct 2025 (art. 37 from 30 Jun 2024) | Deadline passed. **Supersedes Directive 2012/27/EU** — which Directive (EU) 2018/2002 amended — repealed with effect from 12 Oct 2025 (art. 39); cite the recast, never 2018/2002 | `--esg` |
| **(EU) 2026/849** (CRR RTS, delegated) | What counts as an **equivalent legal mechanism** — i.e. a completion guarantee — for a residential property **under construction** under CRR art. 124(3)(a)(iii)(2); decides whether a lender may apply the preferential immovable-property risk weight to an off-plan mortgage | n/a — directly applicable, no transposition; **applies 12 Aug 2026** | Adopted 16 Apr 2026 (C/2026/2429), [OJ L, 2026/849, 23.7.2026](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg_del/2026/849/oj/eng); in force 20th day after publication. Built on EBA final draft EBA/RTS/2025/05 (submitted 5 Aug 2025) + EBA opinion EBA/Op/2026/03 (20 Feb 2026). Verified 2026-07-31 | `--finance` `--type=off-plan` |

**Rule**: For any EU country playbook's `--esg` or `--rental` section, EPBD recast 2024/1275 must be referenced if the section claims any energy-rating rental ban schedule. Since 15 Jul 2026 **no member state has fully transposed it** (all 27 hold a letter of formal notice), so never present an EPBD requirement as if it already bound an owner in a given country — cite the *national* law that exists today, and name the directive only as the source of the obligation to legislate. A national rental-ban schedule that predates the recast (e.g. the FR DPE ladder) stands on its own national footing and is unaffected by this.

**Rule**: The AI Act's high-risk obligations for creditworthiness/credit-scoring systems (annex III, 5(b) → art. 6(2)) apply from **2 Aug 2026**. They bind the *provider and deployer* of the scoring system — a bank, not the buyer — so a `--finance` section may note that a mortgage decision made by an automated system now carries transparency and human-oversight duties, but must not imply the buyer gains a direct claim or that a specific lender is non-compliant.

**Rule**: Reg (EU) 2026/849 is a **bank-side prudential rule, not a buyer obligation** — never present it as something the buyer must satisfy. It is relevant only where a `--type=off-plan` or `--finance` brief discusses mortgage availability: art. 1(2) requires the completion guarantee to be one the **member state's own law requires**, so a jurisdiction with no statutory completion guarantee cannot meet the test at all. Whether a given national scheme (FR *garantie financière d'achèvement*, ES *aval bancario* / *seguro de caución*, IT *polizza fideiussoria*, …) satisfies **all** of art. 1(2)–(13) is the lender's assessment — do not assert that it does.

## Per-playbook impact map

When a Tier 1–2 entry is added here, these playbook sections need re-stamping:

| Topic | Sections to re-stamp |
|---|---|
| tax (rate/threshold) | `--tax`, `--rental` if rental-affecting, country playbook benchmark tables |
| visa (open/close) | `--visa`, country playbook `--work` if tied, `shared/visa-programs.md` |
| rental (regulation) | `--rental`, `--tax` if cedolare-style regime |
| esg (energy-rating) | `--esg`, `--rental` (rental-ban schedules) |
| ownership (foreign buyer) | country playbook `--tax` ownership block, `shared/exit.md` foreign-buyer-pool table |
| eurozone | `--currency`, `--tax`, all country benchmarks in local currency |
| cadastre | country playbook `--mains` + `--tax`, `shared/comparable-transactions.md` tier classification |
| pension regime | `shared/retirement.md`, country playbook `--tax` |

## Maintenance cadence

This file gets the same auto-validate treatment as country playbooks:

- **Weekly**: URL liveness on every entry (cron via `auto-validate.md`)
- **Monthly**: scan all entries with `revisit_by` ≤ 30 days from today; create review queue
- **Quarterly**: full re-research of all Tier 1 entries (visa programs especially); confirm ENDED programs haven't been resurrected
- **At-event**: when a regulatory news item lands (EU Official Journal, national gazette, ECJ ruling, central-bank decision), add or update an entry **before** it propagates errors into playbooks

Subscribe these feeds for at-event updates:
- EU Official Journal: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/oj/direct-access.html
- ECJ press releases: https://curia.europa.eu/juris/recherche.jsf?language=en
- Per-country gazettes (LégiFrance, Gazzetta Ufficiale, BOE, etc. — already in country playbooks)
- OECD Tax Policy Reforms annual report
- Tax Foundation Europe weekly digest

---

## Format for new entries

```markdown
- `<effective_date> | <topic> | <one-line summary> | <source name> | <verified_date> | <revisit_by> | <tier> | <sections>`
```

Multi-line is fine if the entry is complex; the seven-pipe format is the canonical machine-readable form.

- `shared/visa-programs.md` — universal `--visa` section; ENDED registry above is the source of truth
- `shared/auto-validate.md` — cron infrastructure that re-checks URLs in this file weekly
- `shared/exit.md` — foreign-buyer-pool tables that depend on Tier 1 ownership entries
